Session 019ffaac-373a-7661-b332-c539269d210a · /home/akhil/warchives/fde-role · 196 lines · model in file: google/gemini-flash-latest · emulated wire: openai/gpt-4.1 (openai-responses → POST https://api.openai.com/v1/responses) · pi v1.0.2 · All mappings computed by importing pi's shipped functions (no reimplementation). System prompt + tool schemas are not on disk — they are rebuilt at runtime and therefore excluded from wire byte counts below (real requests are larger).

0 · Headline proportions

final wire / disk byte ratio (transcript only)
95 calls
one LLM request per assistant message; 3 user msgs; 92 toolResults; 0 system lines on disk
Why wire < disk here, yet bills more: per request the model sees only text/tool-call/tool-output strings (metadata stripped), so the final context (297,864 B) is smaller than the whole file (441,808 B, which accumulates 95 responses' metadata). But the API resends the growing transcript on every one of the 95 calls, totalling 15,638,416 B across the wire — the number that actually drives cost/latency. The runtime system prompt + tool definitions (absent from disk) are prepended to every request on top of these counts.

2 · Call stack actually executed (imports used by the generator)

  1. pi-coding-agent/dist/core/session-manager.js :: loadEntriesFromFile() → buildSessionProjection(entries, leafId) → sessionEntryToContextMessages() — leaf-path + compaction/context-edit aware projection. Non-message entries (model_change, thinking_level_change, custom:background-tasks-state) project to zero messages.
  2. pi-coding-agent/dist/core/messages.js :: convertToLlm() — AgentMessage[] → LLM Message[] (identity for user/assistant/toolResult here; custom/compaction/branch rewrites only if present — none in this session).
  3. pi-agent-core/dist/agent-loop.js :: streamAssistantResponse() — calls convertToLlm(), then normalizeContext(), then provider stream(model, llmContext). Turn loop: assistant → execute tools → toolResult → next request.
  4. pi-ai/dist/utils/transcript.js :: normalizeContext() (folds runtime systemPrompt+tools into leading system msg) → resolveTranscript(ctx, false) collapses mid-convo system for OpenAI.
  5. pi-ai/dist/api/transform-messages.js :: transformMessages(msgs, gpt-4.1, normalizeId) — cross-provider rules that fired here: thinking→text, strip thoughtSignature, sanitise tool ids, synthesise "No result provided" for orphans, drop stopReason:error/aborted.
  6. pi-ai/dist/api/openai-responses-shared.js :: convertResponsesMessages(model, ctx, {openai,openai-codex,opencode}, {supportsMidConvoSystemMessages:false}) — user→input_text, text→output_text, toolCall→function_call, toolResult→function_call_output. Then buildParams() adds tools/instructions → POST /v1/responses.

3 · Field-level keep / drop (OpenAI Responses, cross-model Gemini → GPT-4.1)

stored on disksent?how
user.content[].text✅input_text, surrogate-sanitised; empty texts dropped
assistant.content thinking✅ as textno OpenAI reasoning passthrough (Gemini sig isn't JSON reasoning) → plain output_text via transformMessages
assistant.content text (non-empty)✅output_text
assistant.content text ""➖empty assistant text still emits an (empty) message item in Responses path; Completions path would drop it
toolCall {id,name,arguments}✅function_call{call_id,name,arguments:JSON}; id sanitised ≤64ch; thoughtSignature deleted
toolResult.content text✅function_call_output{call_id,output:string}; images→input_image only if model takes images
timestamp, responseId, provider/api/model, stopReason/rawStopReason, usage{…}, cost, isError, toolName(on output)❌pi-only bookkeeping; isError not encoded in output string
model_change / thinking_level_change / custom(state)❌project to zero messages (settings/state, not context)
system prompt + tool schemas✅ but absent from diskrebuilt by AgentSession._rebuildSystemPrompt/_preparePromptAndToolLoadout from skills/context-files/tools each run

A blocked link stays in this file with `last_attempt`, the precise `blocker`, and the smallest `next_action`.\n* `inbox/blocked/`: source files that could not be processed safely, each with an adjacent Markdown explanation stating the precise blocker and the smallest action needed to resolve it.\n* `project/sources/raw/<source-id>/`: successfully captured original source files.\n* `project/sources/records/<source-id>.md`: source concepts.\n\nFolder location is the status for source files. The explicit tag in `inbox/links.md` is the status for links. Do not maintain a second manual status register.\n\n# Source IDs\n\nA `source-id` is `<yyyymmdd>-<system>-<slug>`, where the date is the source's occurrence date (fall back to capture date if unknown), `<system>` is `out

    "call_id": "call_309504",
    "output": "---\ntype: Playbook\ntitle: Inbox intake\ndescription: Rules for processing project source files and links from the inbox queue.\ntags: [inbox, source-processing]\ntimestamp: 2026-07-17T00:00:00Z\n---\n\n# Objective\n\nTurn low-effort source intake into reviewable, source-grounded project knowledge. The user supplies a file or link; the AI extracts metadata and creates project records.\n\n# Queue state\n\n* `inbox/`: the sole source queue. It may contain source files and `links.md`, which lists pending or blocked Teams, Outlook, SharePoint, OneDrive, web, or direct-file links.\n* `inbox/links.md`: link entries use `[pending]` or `[blocked]`. A blocked link stays in this file with `last_attempt`, the precise `blocker`, and the smallest `next_action`.\n* `inbox/blocked/`: source files that could not be processed safely, each with an adjacent Markdown explanation stating the precise blocker and the smallest action needed to resolve it.\n* `project/sources/raw/<source-id>/`: successfully captured original source files.\n* `project/sources/records/<source-id>.md`: source concepts.\n\nFolder location is the status for source files. The explicit tag in `inbox/links.md` is the status for links. Do not maintain a second manual status register.\n\n# Source IDs\n\nA `source-id` is `<yyyymmdd>-<system>-<slug>`, where the date is the source's occurrence date (fall back to capture date if unknown), `<system>` is `outlook`, `teams`, `sharepoint`, `onedrive`, `web`, `local`, or `other`, and `<slug>` is a short lowercase hyphenated title. Example: `20260715-teams-data-platform-standup`. IDs are stable once assigned; never rename one.\n\n# Steps\n\n1. Inspect every pending file and `[pending]` link. Retry a `[blocked]` link only when new access, evidence, or user input addresses its recorded blocker. Do not modify the user’s source system.\n2. Derive available metadata: source system, title or subject, author/owner, participants where relevant, occurrence date, captured date, durable URL or message identifier, and access classification where known. Do not require manually entered metadata that the source provides.\n3. Create a `source-id` per the format above. For cloud sources, retain provider item/message identifiers as well as the local ID.\n4. Follow the [source lifecycle](source-lifecycle.md) and [link retrieval](link-retrieval.md) rules to capture the evidence.\n5. Create a source record from the [source record template](../source-management/source-record-template.md). State uncertainty instead of inventing metadata.\n6. Select and complete the relevant [extraction template](../source-management/extraction-templates/) before creating derived knowledge. Use the conflict and uncertainty template when material claims cannot be reconciled safely.\n7. For transcripts, apply the [transcript notes](transcript-notes.md) rules.\n8. Reconcile the source against every affected project artifact: people, notes, resolutions, workstreams, deliverables, proposals, indexes, and `README.md`. Keep project-specific people information in the authoritative `project/people/<person-slug>.md` record. If a person relationship is created or its project slug changes, maintain the corresponding relative symlink at `~/warchives/people-directory/<person-slug>/projects/<project-slug>.md`; do not copy the relationship record into the shared directory. Create or update derived notes when the source warrants them, while preserving the source record as evidence. Where the source implies changes to project knowledge, write a proposal per the [proposal rules](proposals.md); apply only explicit user instructions directly. Set `review_state: review-required` in the frontmatter of any existing concept whose basis the source materially changes.\n9. Move successfully processed input files from `inbox/` into `project/sources/raw/<source-id>/v001/`. If the original filename ends in `.md`, append `.raw` to the stored filename while preserving its bytes and record the original filename in the source record. A `.md.raw` file is immutable source evidence, not an OKF concept: never add frontmatter, index it, rename it back, or rewrite it. Remove successfully processed links from `inbox/links.md`.\n10. Move blocked source files to `inbox/blocked/` with their adjacent explanation. For a blocked link, change its tag to `[blocked]` and record `last_attempt`, `blocker`, and `next_action` on the same line. Never silently discard, overwrite, or infer missing source content.\n11. Add an entry to `project/log.md` summarising the processed and blocked items, then commit the reviewed change set.\n"

It provides provenance, collaboration context, and a refresh target.\n* **Captured local version**: the exact file or text snapshot in this bundle. It is the evidence used for normal project analysis, drafting, and citation.\n\nUse the latest captured local version for normal work. Do not substitute current cloud content because a link resolves. Cloud documents can be edited, moved, or replaced after capture.\n\n# Related source-management rules\n\n* [Source record standard](../source-management/source-record-standard.md) defines the required provenance, version, and refresh-state fields.\n* [Snapshot storage](../source-management/snapshot-storage.md) defines immutable local-version storage.\n* [Source record template](../source-management/source-record-template.md) is the starting structure for a new source record.\n\n# Capture and versioning\n\nFor cloud sources, retain stable provider identifiers when available, such as site, drive, item, message, version, or eTag identifiers, plus the original URL. Prefer identifiers over URLs for refresh.\n\nStore snapshots without overwriting prior evidence:\n\n```text\nsources/raw/<source-id>/v001/<original-filename>\nsources/raw/<source-id>/v002/<original-filename>\n```\n\nWhen the original filename ends in `.md`, append `.raw` to its stored name\nwithout changing its bytes. Record the original filename in the source record.\nThis keeps captured evidence out of the OKF concept namespace.\n\nThe matching source record in `project/sources/records/` lists every captured version in its version-history table; the top row is the latest and the single authority. A local-only source has no cloud origin. A cloud-only reference that cannot be retrieved is pending or blocked, not captured evidence.\n\n# Refresh\n\nRefresh only when explicitly requested: “refresh source <source-id>”, “refresh inbox sources”, or equivalent.\n\n1. Resolve the cloud origin using stored provider identifiers first, then the URL.\n2. Compare remote version metadata and, where practical, content hash against the latest captured version.\n3. If unchanged, record the check time in the source record. Do not create a duplicate snapshot.\n4. If changed, capture a new `v00n` snapshot, update the source record, and summarise material changes.\n5. Identify dependent notes, resolutions, workstreams, and deliverables. Set `review_state: review-required` in their frontmatter and write a proposal per the [proposal rules](proposals.md); do not apply changes silently. Do not add `verified` merely because a source was refreshed.\n6. If the cloud item moved or became inaccessible, retain local evidence and mark the cloud origin `moved`, `unavailable`, or `access-changed`.\n\nNever rewrite a decision or deliverable solely because a source changed.\n\nProject people records remain authoritative in the project repository. A\nshared `people-directory/<person-slug>/projects/<project-slug>.md` entry is\nonly a relative symlink for cross-project navigation. Refreshing a source may\nchange the project-local relationship record, but must not copy that content\ninto the shared directory or replace the canonical stable identity record.\n\n# Freshness and verification\n\nSet `stale_after` only on concepts whose useful life has a known absolute end\ndate, such as recurring status summaries or time-sensitive source records. A\nstale concept requires review; it never authorizes an automatic cloud refresh\nor replacement of local evidence.\n\nUse `verified` only for an evidence-based human review or deterministic\nprocess check. `review_state` tracks whether evidence changes require user\nreview and remains independent of verification.\n"
